── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
material axis absent on 8 cell(s) across 8 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, mid-dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive. See the per-cell notes — absent means not identifiable on that scene, never zero.
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 11 cell(s) across 11 scene(s): impulse__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, impulse__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, impulse__rrect-ml__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard.json

the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 11 cell(s) across 11 scene(s): impulse__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, impulse__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, impulse__rrect-ml__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 45 of 45 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
material axis absent on 8 cell(s) across 8 scene(s): mid-dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive. See the per-cell notes — absent means not identifiable on that scene, never zero.
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 11 cell(s) across 11 scene(s): impulse__rrect-ml__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive, impulse__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard.json

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
material axis absent on 8 cell(s) across 8 scene(s): mid-dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive. See the per-cell notes — absent means not identifiable on that scene, never zero.
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 11 cell(s) across 11 scene(s): impulse__rrect-ml__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive, impulse__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 45 of 45 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard.json

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
material axis absent on 6 cell(s) across 6 scene(s): dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, light-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, light-solid__rrect-md__inactive, light-solid__rrect-ml__inactive. See the per-cell notes — absent means not identifiable on that scene, never zero.
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 13 cell(s) across 13 scene(s): dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, impulse__rrect-ml__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, impulse__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive, impulse__rrect-md__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard.json

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
material axis absent on 6 cell(s) across 6 scene(s): dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, light-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, light-solid__rrect-md__inactive, light-solid__rrect-ml__inactive. See the per-cell notes — absent means not identifiable on that scene, never zero.
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 13 cell(s) across 13 scene(s): dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, impulse__rrect-ml__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, impulse__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive, impulse__rrect-md__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 54 of 54 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard.json
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
material axis absent on 6 cell(s) across 6 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, light-solid__rrect-md__inactive, light-solid__rrect-ml__inactive, light-solid__rrect-lg__inactive. See the per-cell notes — absent means not identifiable on that scene, never zero.
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 13 cell(s) across 13 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, impulse__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__rrect-ml__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, impulse__rrect-lg__inactive, impulse__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard.json
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-2x-light-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
material axis absent on 6 cell(s) across 6 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, light-solid__rrect-md__inactive, light-solid__rrect-ml__inactive, light-solid__rrect-lg__inactive. See the per-cell notes — absent means not identifiable on that scene, never zero.
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 13 cell(s) across 13 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive, impulse__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__rrect-ml__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-sm__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-lg__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, impulse__rrect-lg__inactive, impulse__rrect-md__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 54 of 54 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard.json
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-2x-light-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 3 cell(s) across 3 scene(s): dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard.json
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast (native increased-contrast): reduced-transparency + increased-contrast

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 3 cell(s) across 3 scene(s): dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 19 of 19 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard.json
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast (native increased-contrast): reduced-transparency + increased-contrast

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 3 cell(s) across 3 scene(s): dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
material profile applied to the web side: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/designer/.claude/worktrees/agent-a801e246aa16d039e/packages/calibration/profiles/apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard.json

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 3 cell(s) across 3 scene(s): dark-solid__rrect-80__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-md-clear20__inactive, dark-solid__rrect-48__inactive. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 18 of 18 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
